# Changelog

Changes and updates for all .Net SDKs.

## 2026.9.22

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: a block added to a running pipeline with `AddBlockLive` / `ConnectLive` / `SyncBlockStateLive` could stop the data flow while `SyncBlockStateLive` reported success** - affected the OpenGL effect blocks (`GLResizeBlock` and the rest of the `GL*Block` effects), the H264 encoder, video resize, and file output blocks such as `MP4SinkBlock` (a live-started recording ended without a playable file). Every element of such a block is now started together with it. (#1688)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed: an engine finalized without `Dispose()` logged an `Error` for every dropped instance, even one that was never started** - an app creating many players or capture objects and dropping them could flood the log with identical errors. An instance that had a graph or a start in flight still logs the `Error`; one that never started now logs a `Warning`, and both messages name the source file or capture device to help locate the dropped instance. (#1680)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: removing a video effect from a running capture could freeze the preview until the capture was restarted** - most often at 1080p with a USB camera, with another effect still applied, and logged as `the pipeline did not return to Playing`. Video effects are now added and removed while the pipeline keeps playing, without pausing it. (#1673)
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: `StopAsync()` on the Live Video Compositor, Live Video Compositor V2 and Live Source Switch reported "Unable to stop ... pipeline" errors for pipelines that were not running** — for example when stopping after a failed start (such as an expired trial), calling stop a second time, or when two stops overlapped. Stopping a pipeline that is not running is now handled quietly. (#1676)
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: the OpenGL video effect blocks (`GLResizeBlock`, `GLGrayscaleBlock`, `GLShaderBlock`, and the rest of the `GL*Block` effects) failed to link when placed between regular system-memory video blocks** - the pipeline reported a bare `Noformat` error and never started. The blocks now upload frames to the GPU and download the result back internally, so they work between any video blocks, and non-RGBA input formats (NV12, BGRx) that previously failed even with explicit `GLUploadBlock`/`GLDownloadBlock` are supported. The explicit blocks remain optional, not required. (#1677)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: when a DirectShow filter had no redist file name for the running architecture, creating it threw `ArgumentNullException` (`Parameter name: path2`) instead of reporting the deployment problem.** The engine now logs the usual "Unable to create filter (from file) ... Please check Deployment" error, as it does when the file exists but cannot be loaded. (#1678)
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: an app using the HTTP MJPEG live sink (`HTTPMJPEGLiveSinkBlock`) crashed when a browser client disconnected — for example, when the page showing the stream was refreshed.** The whole process terminated with `System.ObjectDisposedException` instead of just dropping the stale connection; the server now logs the disconnect and keeps serving new viewers. (#1675)

* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: with the EVR or VMR-9 video renderer, a resize, `Video_Renderer_Update()` or video adjustment (ProcAmp) call made while a capture or playback was restarting could log `COM object that has been separated from its underlying RCW cannot be used`.** Such a call is now skipped quietly while the renderer is being rebuilt; the video position and size are applied when the restart completes. (#1560)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Blocks SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Improved: when an Intel Quick Sync (QSV) encoder cannot be created on Windows, the error now names the encoder and the package that provides it** - `VisioForge.CrossPlatform.Core.Windows.Intel.x64` (or `.x86`) - instead of a bare `Unable to create H264 encoder.` (#1670)
* [Demos] The WPF demos that offer QSV encoders now reference `VisioForge.CrossPlatform.Core.Windows.Intel.x64`, so selecting "QSV H264" records instead of failing with `Unable to create H264 encoder.` (#1670)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: with `VideoRendererMode.VideoRenderer` - the legacy DirectShow Video Renderer - the hosting window could not be made always-on-top while video played:** setting `Form.TopMost` (or calling `SetWindowPos` with `HWND_TOPMOST`) reported success and changed nothing. (#1668)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: with the EVR video renderer, shrinking the video area left the previous, larger picture on screen around the new one.** The part of the video window the picture no longer covers is now cleared. (#1654)
* [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed: with `VideoRendererMode.VideoRenderer` - the legacy DirectShow Video Renderer - `StopAsync()`/`Stop()` could take a minute and a half or never return at all, leaving the player impossible to stop or dispose.** Playback with that renderer now starts and stops as quickly as it does with EVR, VMR-9 and Direct2D, whether it was started with `PlayAsync()` or with `Play()`, and whichever thread started it. (#1573, #1659)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: with `VideoRendererMode.VideoRenderer` - the legacy DirectShow Video Renderer - a capture or preview started with `Start()` from a thread that runs no message loop - a thread-pool thread, a worker thread, a console `Main` - could never be stopped or disposed.** `VideoCaptureCore` now builds and tears that graph down on a dedicated message-pumping thread, as `MediaPlayerCore` and `VideoEditCore` already did. (#1658)
* [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed: `VideoRendererMode.VideoRenderer` took the desktop foreground away from the hosting application on every `Video_Renderer_Update()`**, dropping the application window's always-on-top state and letting other windows cover it. The video window is still shown on each update, but no longer changes which window is in front. (#1573)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: with `VideoRendererMode.VideoRenderer` - the legacy DirectShow Video Renderer - the picture was drawn too large and cropped to the video window in an application whose process DPI awareness differs from that of the window it plays into** - a scaled display showed only the top-left part of the frame, which could look like frozen video. The renderer's window is now created and positioned in the DPI awareness context of the window it is given. (#1658)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed: `OSD_Layers_Draw_Image(id, filename, left, top)` drew the image in the top-left corner of the layer with its first `left` x `top` pixels cut off, instead of placing it at (`left`, `top`).** Code that compensated by passing negative offsets must now pass the intended position; code that used the `OSD_Layers_Draw_Image(id, filename, srcRect, destRect, ...)` overload instead is unaffected. (#1570)
* [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed: `Stop()`/`StopAsync()` called while `Play()`/`PlayAsync()` was still opening the source was ignored, and the playback started anyway.** The stop is now carried out as soon as the start finishes: `PlayAsync()` returns `false`, the player ends stopped, and the task returned by `StopAsync()` completes once it has. (#1646)
* [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed: disposing a `MediaPlayerCore` that was still playing never returned**, and neither did its finalizer when the player was dropped without `Dispose()`. The playback is now stopped before the player is released, so `Dispose()` works without a prior `Stop()`. (#1643)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: `Stop()`/`StopAsync()` called while `Start()`/`StartAsync()` was still building the graph tore the graph down while it was being built.** The stop now waits for the build and is carried out when the start finishes, which then returns `false`. (#1646)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed: a Picture-in-Picture source added, deleted or cleared from another thread at the moment `Start`/`Play` began could still slip into the graph build, failing the start with "Unable to set PIP layer settings" or a "Collection was modified" error, and PiP calls made from several threads at once could lose sources.** The PiP source list now changes atomically with the start. (#1569)
* [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed: calling `Play()` on a player that was already playing, or on a paused one to resume it, left it unstoppable - `Stop()`/`StopAsync()` returned without stopping playback, and the Picture-in-Picture sources could be changed under the running graph.** (#1569, #1644)
* [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: `FFMPEGEXEOutput.AddFakeAudioSource` had no effect in `VideoEditCore`.** A job with no audio now gets a silent audio track, as in `VideoCaptureCore`, for both file output and network streaming. `KLVEnabled` does not apply to Video Edit sources and now logs a warning instead of being silently ignored. (#1576)
* [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: in Preview mode, `VideoEditCore.StopAsync()`/`Stop()` could hang for minutes or never return, and `OnStop` never fired; the stop at the end of the timeline could hang the same way.** This now holds for a job started with `Start()` from a thread with no message loop - a thread-pool thread, a worker thread, a console `Main` - as well as for `StartAsync()`. (#1645, #1659)
* [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Changed: `VideoEditCore.Start()` called while a job is already running or starting now reports an error and returns `false` instead of restarting in place.** `StartAsync()` has always refused it; call `Stop()` before starting again. (#1659)
* [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: two `VideoEditCore.Start()`/`StartAsync()` calls issued at almost the same moment - a double-clicked Start button, for instance - could both be accepted and build two jobs over one instance**, giving a truncated or corrupt output file, a preview that could not be stopped, a leaked thread, or a crash. The second call is now always refused, and a job started with `Start()` is always stoppable with `Stop()`. (#1661)
* [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: disposing a `VideoEditCore` while `Start()`/`StartAsync()` was still building the job - closing the window right after pressing Start, for instance - released the objects the build was still using, which could crash the application or leave a job running with no owner.** The release is now carried out as soon as the start finishes, and the start returns `false`. (#1663)
* [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: starting the next job with `Start()` from the `OnStop` handler of a finished conversion - the batch-convert pattern - stalled for ten seconds and was then refused with "Previous graph pump thread is still running".** The next job now starts straight away. (#1664)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: `Dispose()` called while `Start()`/`StartAsync()` was still building the graph - from an `OnStart` or `OnError` handler, or by closing the window right after starting - tore the graph down under the build, after which the start still reported a running capture on the disposed engine; with the legacy `VideoRendererMode.VideoRenderer` started from a thread with no message loop it was instead refused with an error or blocked for about 40 seconds.** The release is now carried out as soon as the start finishes, and the start returns `false`. (#1663)
* [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: converting to `FFMPEGEXEOutput` with `UsePipe = false` recorded only the first few frames of the timeline and reported success.** `VideoEditCore` now always feeds FFMPEG.exe through a pipe and logs a warning when `UsePipe = false`. (#1641)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: `FFMPEGEXEOutput` with the MPEG-4, MPEG-1, MPEG-2, H.263 or FLV1 video encoder failed to start with the default `VideoBufferSize`** ("Either both buffer size and max rate or neither must be specified"). (#1641)
* [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed: with NDI or virtual camera output enabled, `MediaPlayerCore` never raised `OnAudioFrameBuffer`, and the VU meters and audio effects did nothing.** Playback itself was unaffected, and no error was reported. The audio sample grabber now sits in front of every output, so the NDI stream and the virtual microphone carry the same processed audio as the speakers. (#1595)
* [Media Player SDK .Net] [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: the virtual microphone and NDI audio did not work for a source with no video.** `MediaPlayerCore` failed to play an audio-only file (MP3, WAV, AAC, ...) with the virtual camera or NDI output enabled, `VideoCaptureCore` failed to start an audio capture with the virtual camera output enabled, and `VideoEditCore` silently ignored both outputs when previewing a timeline with only audio. All three now send the audio to the virtual microphone or the NDI stream. (#1592)
* [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: with the virtual camera or NDI output enabled, `VideoEditCore` preview played no sound through the speakers when `Audio_Preview_Enabled` was on.** (#1592)
* [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed: with the virtual camera output enabled, `MediaPlayerCore` ignored `Audio_OutputDevice_Volume_Set` and `Audio_OutputDevice_Balance_Set`.** The calls reported nothing and the speakers kept playing at the original level. (#1594)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Changed: `VideoCaptureCore` now respects `MultiScreen_Enabled`.** Screens added with `MultiScreen_AddScreen` are shown only while `MultiScreen_Enabled` is `true`, as in `MediaPlayerCore`; before, they were shown whenever any screen was added, and the property had no effect. (#1561)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: window capture on a scaled display (125%, 150%, ...) captured only the top-left part of the window, enlarged.** `ScreenCaptureMode.Window` now captures the whole window whichever thread starts the capture. (#1561)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: closed captions were not shown while `VideoCaptureCore` multiscreen was on.** (#1561)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: `VideoCaptureCore` showed and recorded no video from a BDA/DVB source (`BDAPreview`/`BDACapture`).** The tuner's stream now reaches the preview and the output, with or without audio. (#1589)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: `SeparateCapture_StopAsync` could never return with `MP4HWOutput` using Intel Quick Sync, leaving the recording unfinished.** The Quick Sync H.264 and HEVC encoders could stop accepting frames mid-recording; they no longer do, and an encoder that stops accepting frames for any other reason now delays the stop by a few seconds instead of hanging it. (#1622)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: with Separate Capture enabled on `MP4HWOutput`, `OnError` could fire "encoder is null" for every frame between the preview start and the first `SeparateCapture_StartAsync`.** (#1622)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: an `FFMPEGEXEOutput` pipe output with `KLVEnabled` and a muxer that cannot carry KLV (for example, FLV for RTMP streaming, or MP4) recorded nothing.** KLV metadata is kept with the MPEG-TS, RTP/MPEG-TS and HLS muxers; with any other muxer the video and audio are now recorded without it and a warning is logged. (#1581)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: with network streaming through `FFMPEGEXEOutput`, the stream ignored the settings of `Network_Streaming_Output`.** It was encoded with FFmpeg's default codec for its container (Sorenson `flv1` for RTMP) at the source resolution, or with the file's settings when both used the same container. The stream now uses its own encoder, bitrate, resize and `Custom_Additional*Args`. (#1579)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: stopping an `FFMPEGEXEOutput` pipe output could occasionally raise `OnError` with "The handle is invalid", although the recording had finished normally.**
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: stopping a hung `FFMPEGEXEOutput`/VLC.EXE task could report the stop as done while the ffmpeg.exe/vlc.exe process still ran and held the output file locked.** The stop now waits for the killed process to actually exit before reporting completion. (#1598)
* [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: with network streaming through `FFMPEGEXEOutput`, the stream used the container and encoder settings of the output file instead of `Network_Streaming_Output`.** (#1579)
* [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Added: `FastEdit_CutFileAsync` can seek before opening the input, which makes cutting from a long file fast wherever the cut starts.** Pass `FastEditSeekMode.Input` as the new fifth argument; the four-argument overload keeps the previous behavior, where cutting near the end of a multi-hour MPEG-TS recording read the whole file up to the cut. For MPEG-TS input (`.ts`, `.m2ts`, `.mts`) the cut starts on the keyframe preceding the requested start, so the output is decodable from its first frame and audio and video stay in sync. The Video Edit demos and agent skills show the new mode for transport streams. (#1623)
* [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: an operation stopped with `FastEdit_StopAsync` was reported as failed with an error ("No process is associated with this object").** A stopped cut, join, audio extraction or mux now raises `OnStop` with `Successful = false` and no error. The tasks returned by `FastEdit_CutFileAsync`, `FastEdit_JoinFilesAsync` and `FastEdit_ExtractAudioStreamAsync` now carry the operation's result as documented, instead of always `true`. (#1597)

## 2026.9.17

* [Media Player SDK .Net] [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Changed: a resize, crop, aspect-ratio crop or box effect can no longer be removed from a running `MediaPlayerCoreX` or `LiveVideoCompositor` pipeline.** Removing one changes the frame size the graph has negotiated, so the call is now refused with a message instead of silently changing it — the same rule `VideoCaptureCoreX` already enforced. Stop the pipeline, remove the effect, and start again. `LiveVideoCompositor` (both versions) also pauses the pipeline around `Video_Effects_Clear` now instead of removing effects while playing, and `MediaPlayerCoreX` gains `Video_Effects_ClearAsync`, the pausing variant of `Video_Effects_Clear`. (#1612)
* [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: with the virtual camera output enabled, `VideoEditCore.Start` could report success while nothing reached the virtual camera.** A virtual camera filter that could not be created, or could not be connected to the graph, left the preview running without it and the call still succeeded. The failure is now reported and `Start` fails, matching `VideoCaptureCore` and `MediaPlayerCore`. (#1590)

## 2026.9.16

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: an Android app could crash with `NotSupportedException: Unable to activate instance of type …` while a screen-capture, camera or USB-camera source was in use.** The SDK's Java callback and view classes missed the `(IntPtr, JniHandleOwnership)` constructor Xamarin.Android needs to rebuild the managed side after the garbage collector has taken it, so a callback the system still held — for example `MediaProjection.Callback.OnStop` on projection revocation — could bring the app down instead of firing. The constructors are in place now, and a rebuilt screen-capture callback still reports the projection revocation instead of silently ignoring it. (#1602)
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] [Core] **Fixed: H264 hardware encoding on Android produced sheared, diagonally distorted video whenever the recording resolution was not one the device's encoder naturally aligns.** The Android MediaCodec encoder plugin filled its input buffers with a guessed row stride and never asked the codec which layout it actually uses; Qualcomm and Samsung Codec2 encoders ignore the guessed layout and read rows at their own alignment, so every frame came out shifted and wrapped. The shipped Android runtime now reads the input format back from the codec after configuration and fills the buffers accordingly. `OpenH264EncoderSettings` was never affected. The same guessed layout also broke software H264 encoding outright at widths not divisible by four - 854x480, for one - where the guess asked for more bytes than the encoder's own input buffer holds and the recording failed on its first frame; and that failure was reported by crashing instead of raising an error. Both are fixed. Separately, a software or vendor H264 encoder whose codec reports both a profile and a level could not be used at all on Android - the encoder failed to start with "Unsupported amc level id" - or produced a stream tagged with the wrong level; both are fixed too. The Android HEVC encoder had the same defect twice over - its profile was looked up among the H264 profiles, so Main was reported as Baseline and an HDR10 profile refused the encoder outright, and its tier and level were read from the wrong table entry - and an encoder reporting a level the SDK does not know now keeps working, tagging the stream without that level rather than refusing to start. (#1601)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: `Frame_GetCurrentFromRenderer` could return a corrupt frame.** The returned bitmap was built over a buffer the SDK had already released, so a later draw or save could read freed memory; on the VMR-9 renderer the bitmap also started at the DIB header instead of the pixels, shifting every row, and row stride ignored the DIB 4-byte alignment. The bitmap now owns its pixels, and an unsupported pixel format reports an error instead of throwing. (#1558)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: twenty documented video effects could not be added at all.** `SVGOverlayVideoEffect`, `CropVideoEffect`, `AspectRatioCropVideoEffect`, `BoxVideoEffect`, `AgingVideoEffect`, `CircleVideoEffect`, `DiceVideoEffect`, `KaleidoscopeVideoEffect`, `MarbleVideoEffect`, `MovingBlurVideoEffect`, `MovingEchoVideoEffect`, `MovingZoomEchoVideoEffect`, `OpticalAnimationBWVideoEffect`, `Pseudo3DVideoEffect`, `QuarkVideoEffect`, `RippleVideoEffect`, `SMPTEAlphaVideoEffect`, `SquareVideoEffect`, `WarpVideoEffect` and `WaterRippleVideoEffect` were listed for the X engines, but adding one with `Video_Effects_AddOrUpdateAsync` logged "Wrong ID specified" and the effect never reached the pipeline. All twenty are applied now; `SMPTEVideoEffect` is a two-input transition that cannot sit in an effect chain and now says so instead of reporting a wrong ID. An output that records or streams follows a crop's size — a 640x480 capture with a 20 px crop on each edge records 600x440 — and property changes on `AspectRatioCropVideoEffect` or `BoxVideoEffect` take effect on the next start rather than immediately.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Changed: a resize, crop, aspect-ratio crop or box effect can no longer be removed from a running capture.** Removing one changes the resolution the recording was configured for, so the call is now refused with a message instead of silently changing it. Stop the pipeline, remove the effect, and start again.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: an MP4 recording could start, report success and silently drop what it was told to do.** `VideoCaptureCore` read a positive HRESULT from a partially rendered connection as a failure, and ignored the real failures: a crop or resize whose filter did not connect left the encoder on the raw source pin, so the file was written without `Video_Crop` or `Video_Resize` applied; a resize was aligned against the source size rather than the requested one, discarding it; and `Start` could answer success for an input that was never connected. Those cases now fail with a message naming the pin, instead of producing a recording that looks right and is not.
* [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: an MP4 or M4A output could be written without one of its tracks.** `VideoEditCore` was told the stream had been muxed even when the muxer connection failed, so the conversion finished and reported success with the video or audio track missing.
* [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed: `MediaPlayerCore` multiscreen worked for one playback only.** The extra windows added with `MultiScreen_AddScreen` showed video the first time and then kept the last frame of it, because stopping playback disposed their renderers and forgot the screens with them. The screens are remembered now and their renderers are built again on every `Play`, with whatever `MultiScreen_SetParameters`, `MultiScreen_UpdateSize` and `MultiScreen_SetZoom` were last given. One consequence for existing code: when the window behind a screen is destroyed, call `MultiScreen_Clear` and add the screens again — a handle is kept until then.
* [Media Player SDK .Net] [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: `TextOverlayVideoEffect.TimeFormat` was ignored in System Time mode.** The clock overlay element names the format property `time-format`, while the SDK only ever wrote `datetime-format` — the name the timestamp overlay uses — so the format string was dropped without a message and the overlay kept showing its default `%H:%M:%S`. The property is now chosen per element, and a format that cannot be applied is logged. (#1604)
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: saving a snapshot as BMP or GIF crashed the application with a `NullReferenceException` instead of saving the frame.** The Skia build the SDK ships has no encoder for BMP or GIF, and the missing result was used without checking it; PNG, JPEG and WebP were unaffected. BMP is now written by the SDK itself, and a format it cannot write (GIF, ICO, WBMP) is refused with a message naming the supported formats — PNG, JPEG, WebP and BMP — instead of a crash. `VideoCaptureCoreX.Snapshot_Save`, `MediaPlayerCoreX.Snapshot_Save` and `VideoRendererBlock.Snapshot_Save` all behave the same way, and so does `PhotoCaptureView.SavePhotoAsync` on iOS and Windows.
* [Demos] **Fixed: the capture demos offered a GIF snapshot the SDK cannot write.** BMP, PNG and JPEG remain in the snapshot save dialog of the Simple Video Capture and IP Capture demos (WPF and Avalonia).
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: taking photos with `PhotoCaptureView` on iOS and Android retained full-resolution bitmaps until the garbage collector ran.** `TakePhotoAsync` is documented as handing the bitmap to the caller, who must dispose it. (#1610)

## 2026.9.15

* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: stopping a capture or conversion with FFMPEG.EXE output could return while the encoder process was still exiting and still holding the output file**, so an application that reopened, moved or deleted the recording right after the stop could find it locked. The stop now waits for the process to actually release it, and a process slower than that is handed to the background close instead of being walked away from.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed:** an aspect ratio override was applied to the wrong axis for many ratio and window combinations, so instead of being letterboxed inside the window the picture was drawn at full height with its sides cut off — a 16:9 override in a 4:3 window lost 80 pixels from each side.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: the application crashed with `Internal CLR error (0x80131506)` when a capture or playback using the Direct2D video renderer was disposed.** The renderer unloaded the filter's module while the graph could still hold the filter, so the next call into it landed in freed memory and took the process down.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: with network streaming enabled, the FFMPEG.EXE output encoded the streamed copy with the container's default encoder.** The configured video and audio encoders, their bitrates, the output resize and all three `Custom_Additional*Args` properties applied to the local file only and were silently ignored for the stream. Both outputs now get them, on both the pipe and the non-pipe output, whenever the stream uses the same container as the file. A stream on a different container still takes the defaults — it needs its own settings object, which the SDK does not pass down yet.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: a capture written through the FFMPEG.EXE pipe output with `AddFakeAudioSource` enabled produced an unplayable MP4 or MKV.** The silent audio source never ends, so the recording could not be finished by closing the inputs and was ended by killing the encoder instead - before it had written the file's index. Those recordings are now finalized normally; MPEG-TS output was unaffected. Two combinations are not covered yet and still need MPEG-TS output: `KLVEnabled`, and a fully custom FFMPEG command line (`Custom_AllFFMPEGArgs`).
* [Demos] **Fixed:** the two Blazor Hybrid demos (Media Player SDK X and Video Capture SDK X) referenced `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Components.WebView.Wpf` 8.0.0, which pulled in `System.Text.Json` 8.0.0 and with it two high-severity advisories (GHSA-8g4q-xg66-9fp4, GHSA-hh2w-p6rv-4g7w) that a vulnerability scanner would flag. Updated to 10.0.101, matching the demos' `net10.0-windows` target.
* [Core] **Fixed: projects targeting plain `net10.0-windows` (or any other `netX.0-windows` without a Windows version) no longer inherit an `NU1701` warning from the SDK.** `SkiaSharp.Views.WPF`, which ships no matching asset for those target frameworks and was restored through the .NET Framework fallback, is no longer a dependency of `VisioForge.DotNet.Core`; `OpenTK.GLWpfControl`, which only came in with it, is gone from the dependency graph as well. The skinned WPF controls are unaffected and keep working on every Windows target framework.
* [Core] **Changed:** the base class of the skinned WPF control `SkinButton` is now `VisioForge.Core.UI.WPF.Skins.VFSkiaElement` instead of `SkiaSharp.Views.WPF.SKElement`. It exposes the same members (`CanvasSize`, `IgnorePixelScaling`, `PaintSurface`, `OnPaintSurface`); only code that casts a `SkinButton` to `SKElement` needs updating. If your own code used types from `SkiaSharp.Views.WPF` (`SKElement`, `SKGLElement`, `ToWriteableBitmap`, …) and relied on the SDK bringing the package in, add `<PackageReference Include="SkiaSharp.Views.WPF" Version="3.119.4" />` to your project — 3.119.4 is the version the SDK's own SkiaSharp is on, and under Central Package Management the version goes into `Directory.Packages.props` instead. On `netX.0-windows10.0.19041` targets it restores without the `NU1701` warning.

## 2026.9.14

* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed: an `ffmpeg.exe` process could be left running after a capture or conversion with FFMPEG.EXE output ended, with its file never finalized.** Disposing the engine performed none of the orderly shutdown, so whenever the stop failed or was never reached the recorder outlived it and nothing held a handle on it any more. Disposal now closes the recorder itself, giving it a few seconds to finish the file before ending it.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed:** stopping a capture or conversion with FFMPEG.EXE output could hang the calling thread - the UI thread in a desktop application - indefinitely when the encoder did not respond to the quit command. The stop is now bounded and ends the process itself if it overruns.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed:** stopping an RTSP Low Latency or NDI source, or a capture writing through the FFMPEG.EXE pipe output, could crash with an access violation or heap corruption when a frame callback was still running while the graph was being disposed, and the RTSP Low Latency source leaked memory on every 32-bit float audio frame. The frame buffers are now freed under a lock the callbacks also hold, so a callback either finishes before the free or sees the stop signal and exits.
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: every pipeline serialization, validation and materialization call threw `MediaBlockType collision: 'GPUPixelBeautyBlock' and 'UDPRAWMPEGTSSourceBlock' both report GPUPixelBeauty`,** so no pipeline document could be saved or loaded and both blocks were unreachable through the block catalog. The beta `GPUPixelBeautyBlock` discriminator moves from 443 to 445. No saved document can have carried the old number, because every path that reads or writes one threw; but C# bakes enum constants into the calling assembly, so an application compiled against 2026.9.11 must be recompiled - otherwise its own `MediaBlockType.GPUPixelBeauty` comparisons silently test for `UDPRAWMPEGTSSource` instead.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed:** the EVR video renderer leaked one Media Foundation video-processor object - and the EVR mixer behind it - on every start, and the fake video renderer used by the FFMPEG.exe output leaked its display-control object the same way, so memory grew slowly in applications that start and stop repeatedly on one engine instance.
* [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed:** the extra windows added by `MultiScreen_AddScreen` stayed black from the second playback onwards with both the EVR and the VMR-9 renderer, and each of them leaked a whole renderer filter - with, on EVR, its mixer - per playback. Those renderers are re-initialized on every playback rather than recreated, and the filters and interfaces of the previous run were kept and used instead of the new ones.
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: on iOS and Mac Catalyst, audio ran ahead of the video in a recording.** The iOS camera, the iOS and Mac Catalyst screen sources and the iOS broadcast extension timed their frames from their own first frame rather than from the pipeline clock, while a live audio source such as the microphone is timed from the moment the pipeline starts playing. The two timelines were therefore offset by however long the pipeline took to build and start, and the offset stayed for the whole recording.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: `Video_CaptureDevice.Format_UseBest` could pick a video format the graph cannot connect to, so every start failed with `VFW_E_CANNOT_CONNECT`.** On devices offering several subtypes at their top resolution (a Logitech BRIO among them) the pick depended on enumeration order and could land on NV12, and the RAW sample grabber additionally demanded RGB24 whenever the format name named no known subtype or auto selection was on. Selection now prefers MJPG at equal resolution, then any subtype the sample grabber supports, the chosen format is logged, and the grabber connects to whatever the device offers instead of failing to connect.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed:** disposing a video renderer or a DirectShow filter twice - or having one collected while the capture graph was being torn down - could release the same COM object twice, reported as `RaceOnRCWCleanup` or `COM object that has been separated from its underlying RCW cannot be used`, and occasionally as a crash during shutdown. Cleanup now runs exactly once per object and never from the finalizer thread.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] [Video Edit SDK .Net] **Fixed:** the same double-release could still be reported from the BDA/DVB source, the Decklink device list, the timeline objects, the Picture-in-Picture sources, the virtual camera streamer and the device enumerator, all of which released COM interfaces and disposed child filters from the garbage collector's finalizer thread. None of them do any more.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed:** an application that dropped a capture or player without calling `Dispose()` could freeze on exit. Cleaning up such an instance ran a synchronous refresh of the video view - and, in the player, a wait for the garbage collector - on the finalizer thread, which never returns once the window it refreshes is gone. Neither step runs there any more, and the player now logs that `Dispose()` was missed, as the capture already did.
* [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed:** a Picture-in-Picture file source never appeared unless the LAV source mode was selected - the wrong filter was asked for its output pin, and the resulting error was swallowed.
* [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed:** each start rebuilt the Picture-in-Picture source filters without releasing the previous ones, so memory grew with every start/stop cycle that used PiP. The renderer input pin each source connects to was never released either. The Video Capture SDK was not affected - it releases its PiP sources at stop.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `OSD_Layers_Draw_Image()` threw instead of returning `false` when the image file was missing or unreadable, so a logo path that had moved took the application down rather than being reported.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed:** every OSD layer method accepted a layer id one past the last layer, and a negative one, instead of rejecting it - `OSD_Layers_Clear(id)` threw out of the call rather than logging the wrong id.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `OSD_Layers_Clear()` and `OSD_Layers_Delete()` dropped an OSD layer without releasing its bitmap, despite documenting that they did; an application that rebuilt its OSD repeatedly held one full-frame 32-bit surface per layer until the next garbage collection. Note that a bitmap obtained from `OSD_Layers_GetBitmap()` is now disposed with its layer, so it must not be used after the layer is cleared or deleted.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `PIP_Sources_Clear()` and `PIP_Sources_Delete()` dropped a Picture-in-Picture source without releasing its filters, so memory and device handles grew every time the sources were reconfigured. The Media Player also kept them until the process exited. They now release what they remove - and, because those filters are connected in the graph while it runs, the whole PiP source list declines and logs instead of changing anything while a capture or playback is in progress. Changing it mid-run never took effect anyway: the sources are read when the graph is built.
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: on Android, audio ran ahead of the video in a recording.** The camera, screen and playback-capture sources timed their frames from their own first frame rather than from the pipeline clock, while a live audio source such as the microphone is timed from the moment the pipeline starts playing. The two timelines were therefore offset by however long the pipeline took to build and start - a fraction of a second with a camera, a couple of seconds with a hardware encoder and screen capture - and the offset stayed for the whole recording. Windows was not affected.

## 2026.9.13

* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed: closing or disposing a WinForms application froze it for 10 seconds while a capture was stopping.** Disposing `VideoCaptureCore` while a `StopAsync` was still running deadlocked the UI thread against the stop thread until an internal timeout expired, after which the graph was released while the stop was still using it. The freeze is gone and the stop now completes in well under a second.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed:** calling `Start()` while a previous stop was still finishing could tear down and rebuild the capture graph from two threads at once, producing `COM object that has been separated from its underlying RCW cannot be used` errors and unstable behaviour afterwards. `Start()` now refuses with a clear message instead; await `Stop()`/`StopAsync()` before starting again.
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Changed:** starting capture from inside an `OnStop` handler is now refused and reported, instead of appearing to succeed and leaving a capture the engine could no longer stop. Start after `Stop()`/`StopAsync()` has returned.
* [Core] **New:** on Windows, WASAPI (1) audio endpoints (`wasapisrc` / `wasapisink`) can be listed alongside WASAPI2 by setting `DeviceEnumerator.Shared.LegacyWasapiDeviceProviderEnabled = true` before enumerating. Off by default — both APIs describe the same physical endpoints, so enabling it lists each one twice, tagged `[WASAPI]` and `[WASAPI2]`.

## 2026.9.12

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] [Video Capture SDK X .Net] [Media Player SDK X .Net] **Fixed: the Uno `VideoView` stopped responding to `Visibility` after a pipeline restart.** Starting a pipeline forced the control back to `Visibility="Visible"`, overwriting whatever the application had bound to it. Since the bound source property itself had not changed, no later binding update put it back, so from that point the view could never be collapsed again - it stayed on screen with a frozen frame and hid whatever the page drew underneath it. This showed up most often after leaving a page and returning to it, where the pipeline is rebuilt while the application already considers the view hidden. The control no longer writes its own `Visibility`; showing and hiding it is the application's decision.
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** collapsing the Uno `VideoView` now hides the native video surface on iOS and Mac Catalyst, where it is a window-level overlay outside the XAML tree and previously kept drawing over the page.
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** leaving a page that holds a Uno `VideoView` on Android now releases the native view. Its render thread and frame buffers used to stay alive for the rest of the process, once per navigation.

## 2026.9.11

* [NuGet] Release packaging now verifies that fixed-cadence native packages are locally prepared and published before the release is signed.
* [Demos] iOS and Mac Catalyst demos now declare local-network usage, so streaming or playback to a LAN host prompts for permission instead of failing silently (#829).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** a stalled SRT MPEG-TS sink no longer freezes the rest of the pipeline, including camera preview (#829).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **New (beta):** `GPUPixelBeautyBlock` applies GPU skin smoothing and whitening. Reference `VisioForge.CrossPlatform.GPUPixel.Windows.x64`. Face slim, eye enlarge, lipstick and blusher are no-ops on Windows until a face detector ships (#1495).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** a `VideoResizeBlock` (or a `ResizeVideoEffect`) no longer drops the stream when the source changes frame size mid-take. A 90° rotate, a device orientation change or an adaptive stream switching resolution used to end the recording after one frame (#1514).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** adding or removing a LUT effect while a pipeline is playing no longer leaves it stuck short of PLAYING (#1513).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: `RTMPSourceBlock` delivered no video.** An RTMP stream that carried audio left the reader stalled a moment after connecting - the connection succeeded, `flvdemux` parsed the FLV header and the AVC sequence header, and then nothing: the video output pad was never created and a recorder behind the block wrote an empty file. The block now links the demuxer's video and audio streams to its outputs as they appear and discards any stream the caller left unconnected, so video flows and `GetVideoStreamInfo()` reports the real frame size (it returned `0x0` on a live source) (#1489).
* [NuGet] CrossPlatform GStreamer redistributable packages are now 2026.9.11 (GStreamer 1.28.6) for Windows, Apple, Android and Linux.

## 2026.9.10

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `NDISourceXBlock` never delivered a stream and prevented the process from exiting. Source settings now apply; enumerating sources no longer breaks a running connection; receiving audio no longer leaks memory (#1490).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] [Video Capture SDK X .Net] **Fixed:** a frame-size change during recording (for example an Android rotation) now raises `OnError` and stops that output instead of silently corrupting the file. Call `StopAsync` / `StopCaptureAsync` when you get it. Live streaming, MPEG-TS and preview are unchanged (#1496).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] [Video Capture SDK X .Net] [Video Edit SDK X .Net] **Changed:** H.264 outputs now default to the platform hardware encoder on iOS, macCatalyst, macOS and Android. Construct the output after `InitSDK`. Pass an `IVideoEncoder`, or set `Video`, to keep software H.264 (#1505).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** text overlays on Android now use fonts bundled with the application. Name the font family as recorded inside the file, not the file name (#1500).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] [Video Capture SDK X .Net] [Video Edit SDK X .Net] **New:** `GetVideoEncoders()` now lists Android and Apple hardware encoders. On Android an entry appears only when the device actually provides that encoder (#1499).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `AndroidAV1EncoderSettings` auto-detection now finds the device's AV1 encoder (#1499).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** choosing "AMF H265" in an encoder picker no longer throws `MissingMethodException` (#1499).

## 2026.9.9

* [Video Capture SDK X .Net] [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **New:** the Android camera can follow device rotation. Call `Video_Source_UpdateOrientation()` / `UpdateOrientation()`, or set `AutoUpdateOrientation = true`. Default is unchanged. Turn automatic mode off around a recording — a 90° turn swaps width and height, which a recording already in progress cannot follow (#297).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `SilenceDetectorBlock` could report a silence period ending in the year 2554. `Timestamp` is now `TimeSpan?` and is `null` when the position is unknown. **Breaking:** code reading `Timestamp`, or `Period` on `OnSilenceEnded` without a null check, needs updating.
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `OnSilenceEnded` from `FinalizeSilencePeriods` now reports the last measured level, not the threshold, and no longer records a period whose end precedes its start.
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `TextOverlayBlock` crashed when `Settings` or `Font` was `null`. Null settings now pass video through with no overlay (#1497).

## 2026.9.8

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Added:** `EbuR128LevelBlock.OnMeasurement` reports momentary, short-term and integrated loudness, relative threshold and loudness range (#1442).
* [Core] **Fixed:** an application that did not call `VisioForgeX.DestroySDK()` never exited after building a pipeline. `DestroySDK()` is still the right way to release the SDK (#1448).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** playing an audio file through `AudioRendererBlock` played a few frames then fell silent, and stopping took about ten seconds (#1464).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** stopping a pipeline that writes no file no longer raises a warning about a missing trailer (#1464).

## 2026.9.7

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** stopping a RIST output took about 10 seconds (#1463).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** a detector added to a running pipeline with `AddBlockLive` never raised its event. Removing it live now also stops its events (#1473).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `VUMeterBlock` could not be connected to `AudioMixerBlock` (`Unable to link pads ... Error Noformat`).
* [Media Player SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `MediaPlayerCore` could crash playback with an access violation when Clear unloaded a DirectShow filter still in use (#1471).
* [Core] **Fixed:** DirectShow filter DLLs could be unloaded or freed twice during teardown, crashing the next graph (#1462).

## 2026.9.6

* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `VideoCaptureCore.Clear()` could crash the next capture with an access violation by unloading a DirectShow filter still in use (#1455).
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `Audio_OutputDevice_Volume_Get` blocked the calling thread and `Audio_OutputDevice_Volume_Set` applied the volume late when started with `StartAsync` (#1454).
* [Core] The `AutoH264EncoderSettings`, `AutoHEVCEncoderSettings` and `AutoAV1EncoderSettings` types, announced in 2026.4.21, were removed in 2026.4.30. Choose a per-runtime settings class (AMF, NVENC, QSV, Media Foundation, D3D11/D3D12 or software) and check `IsAvailable()` before building the pipeline.

## 2026.9.5

* [ALL] Anonymous error telemetry works again while you develop against the SDK. It runs only with a debugger attached; a shipped application never sends anything. Reports go to `https://telemetry.visioforge.org` and contain no file paths, device identifiers, media or configuration. Set `Debug_Telemetry = false` to switch it off.

## 2026.9.4

* [Core] **Fixed:** `Gst.Uri.QueryTable` and `Gst.Uri.MediaFragmentTable` threw `ArgumentException` on any URI that had a query or fragment.
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** stopping a pipeline parked in READY after a natural end-of-stream (`PauseOnStop`) could start it playing again.
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `Stop` could report success over an unplayable recording when the graceful wait expired. The truncation is now logged as an error.
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** a recording could lose its trailer after an errored source was removed from a running pipeline.
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `SendEOS` could throw `NullReferenceException` when `Dispose` ran at the same time.
* [Core] **Fixed:** the MAUI Skia, Apple and Windows video views ignored the background color you set (#301).
* [Core] **New:** `VideoView.BackgroundColor` on WinUI and Android (`VideoViewTX`) (#301).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** stopping a pipeline whose source had errored before the first frame waited ~23 seconds. Such a stop now completes promptly; recordings that were playing still get their trailers (#1382).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** a live add whose build failed left its elements inside the running pipeline (#1409).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `DemuxerSourceBlock` published a silent subtitle pad for streams it never fed (#1414).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `DemuxerSourceBlock` hung at start on MPEG-TS files and barely played MOV files (#1425).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** unconnected output pads stalled the streams you did connect. Set `OnDemandConnect` on a pad you intend to connect after the build (#1415).

## 2026.9.3

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `LiveVideoCompositor` (V2) could not be stopped and started again. `GetVideoMixer()` and `GetAudioMixer()` now return `null` while stopped (#1377).
* [Core] **Fixed:** on macOS, an application embedding the SDK's native plugins could be killed at startup with "Code Signature Invalid". If you notarize an app that embeds `libMoltenVK.dylib`, re-sign it with `codesign --options runtime` (#1371).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** demuxed closed captions, WebVTT, TTML, DVB teletext and ONVIF metadata were discarded instead of reaching an output pad (#1387).
* [Core] **Removed:** `VideoViewUIEngine.iOS_CG` and `IVideoViewPushFrame.PushSample`. Report `Apple_Metal` or `Apple_OpenGL` and render through `PushFrame` (#1401).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Changed:** `VideoRendererBlock` now fails with `NotSupportedException` when the video view's UI engine is unrecognised (#1401).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** live Whisper subtitles ran ahead of the picture. Captions now follow the frame timestamp. `CaptionTimeline` is public for driving your own caption label (#822).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **New:** play at normal speed with live captions. Split audio with a `TeeBlock` so one branch plays while the other feeds speech-to-text. See the Live Subtitles guide (#822).
* [Demos] File-playback speech-to-text demos gained a "Real-time playback" switch, on by default (#822).
* [Core] **Fixed:** on iOS, reading a GStreamer format or metadata descriptor, or implementing a GStreamer/GLib interface, aborted the application (#1391).

## 2026.9.2

* [Core] **Fixed:** on iOS, any pipeline with a sample grabber, custom transform or custom colorspace crashed or failed to build (#820).
* [Core] **Changed:** on Mac Catalyst, audio output device names now carry their API tag (`[CoreAudio] <name>`). Persist `Name` and `UniqueID`, not `DisplayName` (#1397).
* [Core] **Changed:** device display names now follow one rule — the API tag appears only where the platform offers more than one API. On iOS both `DisplayName` and `ToString()` are the bare device name. On macOS and Mac Catalyst `VideoCaptureDeviceInfo.DisplayName` now carries the tag. Persist `Name` (and `UniqueID` / `AvfUniqueID` on Apple), not the display string (#1400).
* [Core] **Changed:** `VideoCaptureDeviceInfo.IsFlashAvailable` and `IsTorchAvailable` are no longer available on Mac Catalyst (#1397).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** chroma key on a video mixer input could not be switched off, and updating one input's chroma key could change another input's (#1366).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** a property set while the pipeline was being stopped from another thread could be silently ignored (#1366).
* [Core] **Fixed:** on macOS, starting any child process while an SRT pipeline was running could hang the application (#1381).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** on iOS, stopping an SRT sink whose peer keeps disappearing could block indefinitely (#1349).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `LiveVideoCompositor` (deprecated V1) reported success when an input failed to start. If you derive from V1 `LVCInput`, `Build` now returns `bool` (#1383).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** restarting `LiveVideoCompositor` (deprecated V1) built a second composition on top of the first (#1388).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** in `LiveVideoCompositor` (deprecated V1), an output added after another was removed took over a live output's stream (#1398).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** a failed `Input_AddAsync` or `Output_AddAsync` on `LiveVideoCompositor` (V2) left the failed input or output inside the compositor (#1384).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** on a `LiveVideoCompositor` (V2) with audio disabled, every video/audio input carrying audio was wired to the same internal audio channel (#1384).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Changed:** `LiveVideoCompositor` (V2) now enforces input and output limits (`MaxVideoInputsCount` and related, default 10). An eleventh add that used to succeed is now refused — raise the setting if needed (#1384).
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed:** DirectCapture from an IP camera could record with an unrelated FFmpeg found on `PATH`, producing video with no audio (#83).
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed:** DirectCapture from an IP camera always recorded the camera's audio. `Audio_RecordAudio` now governs it (#83).
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `DirectCaptureDV` and `DirectCaptureCustom` with an IP camera started, reported success and wrote no file. Both are now refused (#83).
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed:** with separate capture and `SeparateCapture_AutostartCapture = false`, a DirectCapture IP camera capture began writing on `Start()`. Recording now begins at `SeparateCapture_Start` (#83).
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed:** DirectCapture accepted an NDI source and produced nothing. The combination is now refused (#83).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] On macOS, RT-DETR object detection now runs on the CPU by default (`Auto`), using less than half the memory. Set `OnnxInferenceSettings.Provider = CoreML` to keep CoreML (#862).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** disposing an object detector while it was processing a frame could crash the process (#862).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** stopping a pipeline built on `MPEGTSDemuxBlock` or `QTDemuxBlock` could crash, and an extra stream in the file was dropped (#1365).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** removing an output from a running Live Video Compositor left a tee branch behind. `TeeBlock.RemovePadLive` is the public counterpart to `AddNewPadLive` (#1367).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `VideoMixerBlock.Inputs` grew without bound on a Live Video Compositor that added and removed video inputs (#1367).

## 2026.9.1

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** changing the background image of `SqueezebackBlockV2` stalled for half a second. The swap now completes in about a frame on a running pipeline (#832).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** the Letterbox mode of `ImageVideoSourceCairoBlock` centred the image on the wrong dimensions (#832).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **New:** `ImageVideoSourceCairoBlock.UpdateFilenameAsync` / `TryUpdateFilename`, and `SqueezebackBlockV2.BackgroundImageFile` (#832).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** `ImageVideoSourceCairoBlock` logged a GStreamer critical every time the pipeline stopped (#832).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** an image overlay fade could crash the application when the pipeline was stopped during the fade. `VideoPaddingChangerBlock.Input_Move` is fixed the same way (#837).
* [Core] **Fixed:** `MediaInfoReaderX` file snapshots could return a frame from the previous file, throw `ArgumentException` instead of `null`, leak temp files, wait out the full timeout on an undecodable file, and hold the captured frame in memory after return (#837).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** a Unity player built with the IL2CPP scripting backend could not use the SDK at all (#820).
* [Demos] **Fixed:** in the Android demos the bottom button row was drawn under the system navigation bar (#1358).
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Removed:** the RTSP X network streaming output (`NetworkStreamingFormat.RTSP_X`, `RTSPXOutput`, and the `VisioForge.DotNet.Core.Redist.RTSPX.x64` package). Use `VideoCaptureCoreX` with `RTSPServerOutput` (#1262).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** on Windows, stopping an SRT pipeline never returned when the receiving peer had gone away (#1190).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** the same SRT stop hang on Android (#1350).
* [Core] The RAV1E AV1 encoder now encodes across CPU cores. `RAV1EEncoderSettings.Tiles` defaults to 16; set `Tiles = 0` for the previous single-tile output (#1345).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed:** a Basler camera was opened by its position in the device list, not by the camera you named (#1344).
* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] Android now ships GStreamer 1.28.6, matching every other platform (#1268).
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed:** a capture that could not build its graph killed the process on stop. Update `VisioForge_MP4_Muxer_v10*.ax` and `VisioForge_AAC_Encoder_v10*.ax` in your redistributables (#841).
* [Video Capture SDK .Net] **Fixed:** stopping or disposing a capture could kill the process with an access violation (#841).

## 2026.8.31

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: a video mixer could save a source that would not load back, and a restored Basler source could report zero frame rate.** A mixer source whose camera name had been cleared was written into the document without it, the snapshot reported success, and loading that document failed - it is refused at save time now, naming the block. And a Basler camera whose frame rate could not be read when the document was saved kept a zero frame rate on every later load, which reached the recording's frame info; it is re-read now, as the sensor size already was.

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: a Spinnaker source could open a different camera than the one it was told to, without saying so.** The camera was addressed by its position in the enumerated list rather than by its own index, so one camera failing to initialize - held by another process, for instance - shifted every camera after it, and the source silently opened its neighbour. A name matching no camera at all, or matching two identical bodies (the name is the model name, which they share), was equally silent. The index is now the camera's own, `SpinnakerCameraInfo` carries the serial number, and all three cases are reported. two handles leaked during enumeration are released; one camera failing to answer no longer hides every other camera on the rig, and no longer leaves a truncated list cached; a camera whose model name cannot be read is listed under its serial number instead of under a name every unreadable camera shares; and a camera index the element cannot address fails the source instead of quietly opening a different camera - the element accepts 0-7, so on a rig with nine or more cameras the ninth and beyond now report an error at build time rather than streaming a neighbour.

* [X-engines] **Fixed: an SDK error killed the process when the application had not subscribed `OnError`.** `VideoEditCoreX` reported such an error by logging it, and the log is what raises the error event, so every error re-entered the reporting path and recursed until the stack overflowed - the process died with no exception and no message. A render that could not build its encoder was the common way in. The error is now reported once, and shown on the video view exactly as the other engines do. `MediaBlocksPipeline` had the same loop on its two "VisioForge_MFP.dll not found" messages, which recursed whenever a debugger was attached (#1173).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: a virtual camera sink with an input that was created but never connected wedged the pipeline.** `VirtualCameraSinkBlock` built the chain for a pad that exists rather than for one that is connected, so an unconnected input left a branch with nothing feeding it - a branch that can neither preroll nor reach End-Of-Stream, leaving either `StartAsync` or `StopAsync` never returning. Two connected inputs of the same type are now reported as an error too - only one of them was ever linked (#1288).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **`NDISinkBlock.IsAvailable()` now reports `false` when the NDI runtime is missing.** It answered from the GStreamer element alone, so on a machine carrying the plugin but no NDI runtime it reported the sink as available and the pipeline then failed to start (#1288).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: stopping a pipeline whose `NDISinkBlock` had only a video input never returned.** `StopAsync` blocked forever and the pipeline could never be disposed; adding an audio input was the only workaround. The sink built its audio chain even when no audio was connected, and the unfed combiner pad stalled the End-Of-Stream a graceful stop sends. A video-only NDI sink now stops normally. An NDI sink with no video input, which cannot produce an NDI stream at all, is now reported as a build error instead of hanging the same way (#1288).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: nothing decoded when a decoder was connected to `UniversalDemuxBlock`.** The demuxer hands out encoded data, so a decoder behind it is the normal way to use it - and that graph delivered no frames at all, while the pipeline reported a successful start and put nothing on the bus. A stream the demuxer was told not to render was sent to a plain null sink, which stalled the demuxer until the pipeline reached playing, and the pipeline could not reach playing because the decoder branch was starved of the frames it needed. This affects `UniversalDemuxBlock` and `UniversalAutoDemuxerBlock` with `renderVideo` or `renderAudio` turned off, and every decoder behind them, `UniversalDecoderBlock`, `DecodeBinBlock` and the codec-specific decoder blocks alike. A demuxer also no longer throws when the file turns out to hold more streams of a type than its media info reported, and `UniversalDemuxBlock` can now output a metadata stream (#836).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **`UniversalDemuxSettings.Type` can now be set.** It was declared without a setter, so that settings class always meant automatic container detection whatever was assigned to it; the per-format settings classes were the only way to pick a specific demuxer. The default is unchanged (#836).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Pipeline documents now write enum settings by name instead of by number.** Many of the SDK's enums declare their members per platform, so the same ordinal meant a different member elsewhere - `1` was `WASAPI` on Windows and `Decklink` on macOS - and a document moved between platforms silently restored the wrong device API, encoder or format. A saved pipeline now reads `"api": "WASAPI"`. Documents written before this still load; a document written by this build cannot be opened by an earlier one (#1322).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **A pipeline document now restores the audio device a block was using.** `AudioRendererBlock`, `SystemAudioSourceBlock` and the WASAPI, WASAPI2, DirectSound, loopback and PulseAudio audio sources hold an `AudioOutputDeviceInfo` or `AudioCaptureDeviceInfo`, and neither could be written to a document and read back - so a saved pipeline came back either refusing to load or holding no device at all. A document now stores what identifies the device (its name, path, API and, on macOS, the stable CoreAudio `unique-id`), and materialization matches that against the devices the machine actually has, handing the block a live device again. This also fixes a saved configuration opening the wrong speaker or microphone: the numeric CoreAudio id and the WASAPI path are both reassigned between runs, and the match now re-reads the current ones. When the named device is gone, the pipeline still builds on a system device of the same API and materialization reports it as the new `MBS063` warning naming both devices. `AlliedVisionSourceSettings` restores from the camera's identity the same way, without opening the camera (#1322).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: a pipeline document silently lost mandatory block settings and restored a block wired to the wrong thing.** A setting exposed as a read-only property - the bridge `Channel` above all, but also `CustomMediaBlockSettings.ElementName`, `GLOverlaySettings.Filename`, `GLEquirectangularViewSettings.VideoWidth`/`VideoHeight`, `AlliedVisionSourceSettings.Camera` and the GenICam region, frame rate and pixel format - was never written into a saved document, and came back as `null` or `0` with validation, deserialization and materialization all reporting success. Two app bridges saved on different channels reloaded onto the same one. Those settings now round-trip, a document missing one is reported as `MBS043` naming the property instead of restoring a mis-wired block, and `MediaBlockDescriptor.Properties` lists them (with the new `IsRequired` and `IsReadOnly` flags) so an editor can author them. Eight settings types could not be restored at all, because a constructor parameter matched no property and the serializer refuses to bind such a constructor - every document holding a `BridgeAudioSource`, `HTTPSource`, `AudioRenderer`, `OpenCVTemplateMatch`, `LiveSourceSwitch`, `LiveSourceSwitchDynamic`, `AlliedVisionSource` or `VideoAspectRatioCrop` block failed to load. All of them now restore. The parameters are renamed to match their properties: `BridgeAudioSourceSettings(channel, info)`, `HTTPSourceSettings(location, isLive)`, `AudioRendererSettings(info)`, `CVTemplateMatchSettings(templateImage)` and `LiveSourceSwitchSettings(videoWidth, videoHeight, videoFrameRate)`. Positional calls are unaffected; code passing these by name must be updated (#1322).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: a pipeline document holding a `SpinnakerSource` block could not be loaded, and a mixer holding a Spinnaker source could not be saved.** The constructor's first parameter matched no property, which the serializer refuses outright, and the region, frame rate and pixel format were never written into the document. All four settings now round-trip. The parameter is renamed to match its property: `SpinnakerSourceSettings(name, region, frameRate, pixelFormat)`. Positional calls are unaffected; code passing the first argument by name must be updated (#1338).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: a pipeline document holding a `GenICamSource` or `BaslerSource` block came back without its camera.** `GenICamSourceSettings` declares two constructors and no parameterless one, which the serializer refuses outright, so no document holding the block loaded at all - while its region, frame rate and pixel format were written into the document regardless. `BaslerSourceSettings.Camera` was never written, so the block restored with no camera while saving, validation and loading all reported success. Both now round-trip from the identity the document carries, and a Basler document that carries the sensor size no longer opens the camera to re-read it. The first parameter of both `GenICamSourceSettings` constructors is renamed `deviceName` -> `name` to match its property; positional calls are unaffected. A `SpinnakerSource` or `GenICamSource` document carrying no camera name, or an empty one, is now reported as `MBS043` instead of silently opening the first camera on the machine (#1338).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: a video mixer holding an Allied Vision or GenICam camera could not be saved.** The mixer wrote a source only when it could read it back, and that check looked at public constructors alone - so it refused a settings class whose deserialization constructor is private, with an `MBS060`, even though loading a document restores it correctly. Both cameras now save and reload as mixer sources. A mixer source missing a mandatory setting - the region above all - is reported now too, where it used to load as a camera with no region and then fail when the pipeline started (#1338).

* [Core] **Fixed: a capture device whose sample rate range lies outside 22050-96000 Hz disappeared from the device's format list.** A narrow-band device publishing, say, `rate=(int)[ 8000, 16000 ]` matched none of the rates the enumeration samples a range at, so it produced no entries at all. Its own bounds are published instead (#1317).

* [Core] **Fixed: a capture device publishing its sample rate or channel count as a list disappeared from the device's format list entirely.** `AudioCaptureDeviceInfo.Formats` read only the single-value and range forms, so a caps structure written as `rate=(int){ 44100, 48000 }` produced no entries at all rather than one per combination (#1317).

* [Core] **Fixed: audio frames delivered as 24-bit-in-32 or as 20-/18-bit reported the wrong bytes per sample.** `AudioFrame.Info.BPS` carried the number of significant bits rather than the container width, so a consumer of `BufferSinkBlock` or `AudioSampleGrabberBlock` read 3 bytes per sample where the buffer holds 4 (`S24_32*`) - a 25% undercount that garbles the audio - and 2 bytes where 20- and 18-bit formats hold 3. `S24BE` reported 0. Unchanged for 8-, 16-, 32- and 64-bit formats, which is everything the SDK produces by default (#1317).

* [Core] **Fixed: 24-bit-in-32 audio formats were dropped from capture-device format lists and could not be requested.** GStreamer writes these as `S24_32LE`/`S24_32BE`/`U24_32LE`/`U24_32BE` while the `AudioFormatX` enum spells them without the underscore, and the SDK converted between the two by name. A device offering one of them lost it from `AudioCaptureDeviceInfo.Formats`, and asking for it anywhere a format is applied - audio capture, the audio mixer, WAV output, a push source, the iOS/Mac audio renderer - produced a format name no element accepts. Both directions are now translated (#1317).

* [Core] **On macOS and Mac Catalyst the default audio capture format now follows the device's own sample rate.** `AudioCaptureDeviceInfo.GetDefaultFormat()` preferred 44100 Hz and, when a device published no 16-bit stereo combination at all, fell back to whichever entry sorted first alphabetically. Since the capture format is applied to the capture device itself, that could ask CoreAudio to run an input at a rate it does not use - CoreAudio cannot resample an input, so the hardware's nominal rate was changed instead. The rate now comes from the format the device reports as its own; 16-bit stereo is still preferred for the sample format and channel count, which CoreAudio does convert. Devices publishing 16-bit stereo at 44100 or 48000 Hz - the common case - keep the format they had (#1317).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **`IsAvailable()` on the video and audio encoders and decoders no longer reports a codec that cannot actually be created.** It answered from the GStreamer registry alone, and the registry caches a plugin's element list while the elements a plugin registers are decided when it loads — the Media Foundation plugin names its encoders by hardware-MFT enumeration index, and the NVIDIA, Intel and Direct3D plugins register their elements only for the hardware present at scan time. After a driver, GPU or Windows-codec change the registry could still list an element the plugin no longer provides; `IsAvailable()` returned `true` and the pipeline then failed to build with an unrelated-looking error. The check now also loads the element's plugin and asks it again, which refreshes the registry's list for that plugin. The first `IsAvailable()` call for a given backend is correspondingly slower, since it now loads that backend's plugin — around 0.3 s for NVIDIA and 0.7 s for Media Foundation on the measured host, once per plugin per process — and that plugin's vendor libraries are loaded into the process even if the pipeline ends up encoding in software (#1175).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **`MFH264EncoderSettings.GetDeviceIDs()` and `MFHEVCEncoderSettings.GetDeviceIDs()` no longer list a Media Foundation device that cannot be created**, and they identify each device from the loaded MFT rather than from the cached registry metadata, which could name a different encoder. Two consequences on a host where such a stale entry existed: a device that used to appear and then fail is now absent, and `DeviceID` — a position in this list — can shift, so an application that persisted a `DeviceID` should re-resolve it against `GetDeviceIDs()` rather than assume last run's number (#1175).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Windows: using a Media Foundation encoder no longer permanently breaks every later NVIDIA NVENC encode in the process.** GStreamer eagerly activated NVIDIA's Media Foundation H.264 and HEVC MFTs while registering the plugin, even when the pipeline only requested the Microsoft AAC encoder. If the public NVENC API had already been initialized, the next `nvh264enc`/`nvh265enc` session failed with `NV_ENC_ERR_INVALID_VERSION` (`0xf`) and no later session could recover. The bundled Media Foundation plugin now excludes NVIDIA's video MFTs before `IMFActivate::ActivateObject`. Microsoft, Intel, AMD, software Media Foundation video encoders, all Media Foundation audio codecs, video capture (`mfvideosrc`) and device enumeration remain available. **What changes on an NVIDIA machine:** NVIDIA's Media Foundation *video* encoders are no longer registered by the plugin. `MFH264EncoderSettings` and `MFHEVCEncoderSettings` keep selecting the same encoder and the same `DeviceID` numbering as before — they already excluded NVIDIA entries when enumerating — **with one exception: a host whose only Media Foundation HEVC encoder was NVIDIA's.** There the settings class enumerated nothing and fell back to the bare `mfh265enc` element, which used to be the NVIDIA one; nothing registers that name now, so `HEVCEncoderBlock.IsAvailable(new MFHEVCEncoderSettings())` returns `false` and `HEVCEncoderType.MF_HEVC` is unavailable. Install the Microsoft HEVC Video Extensions, or encode HEVC through `NVENCHEVCEncoderSettings`. H.264 is not affected in the same way: Windows always registers the Microsoft software H.264 MFT. What changes is the plain GStreamer element: `mfh264enc` and `mfh265enc` are now backed by the next non-NVIDIA MFT on the host — Intel or AMD hardware where present, otherwise the Microsoft software H.264 MFT and the HEVC Video Extension — so a hand-written pipeline string naming them gets a different encoder. Use `NVENCH264EncoderSettings`, `NVENCHEVCEncoderSettings` or `NVENCAV1EncoderSettings` for NVIDIA hardware encoding; they are unaffected and are the faster path in any case (#842).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **`MFAACEncoderSettings` is no longer deprecated.** It was marked obsolete because using it broke NVENC later in the same process; that was never the AAC encoder's doing — requesting it loads the Media Foundation plugin, whose video-MFT registration was the real cause, and that is fixed above. Code using it no longer produces a compiler warning. `AVENCAACEncoderSettings` stays the Windows default for `MP4Output`; prefer `MFAACEncoderSettings` for `VideoEditCoreX` render paths, where libav AAC does not round-trip the GstPreset (#842).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: `AudioRendererBlock.Mute` could not turn audio back on on iOS and Mac Catalyst.** Setting it to `false` wrote volume 0 again, leaving the renderer silent and reporting itself muted. Muting now preserves the configured volume, and unmuting restores audible output (#1318).

* [Core] **Fixed: HTTPS and RTSPS connections on Mac Catalyst could not initialize TLS.** The GStreamer runtime was searched for in a `lib` subfolder that Mac Catalyst app bundles do not contain, so its OpenSSL TLS backend and certificate database were never loaded. The SDK now finds the bundled runtime and verifies TLS peers as on macOS (#1327).

* [Core] **On macOS and Mac Catalyst an application no longer re-scans the whole GStreamer plugin set on every launch.** The scan is now cached in the application's own Caches directory (`Library/Caches/VisioForge/<bundle-id>/gstreamer/`, inside the sandbox container for a sandboxed app) and paid once - on the first start after installing or updating the application, and after that only for plugins whose files changed. Nothing needs to be deployed or configured; deleting the cache folder costs one more scan (#1299).

* [Core] **On macOS and Mac Catalyst `GST_PLUGIN_PATH` is no longer set in the .NET environment.** It is published to the process environment GStreamer itself reads, which the .NET one on Unix never was - so `Environment.GetEnvironmentVariable("GST_PLUGIN_PATH")` now returns nothing on these platforms, and a child process started by the application no longer inherits the application's own plugin directory. Pass it explicitly to a child that needs it (#1299).

## 2026.8.30

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **Fixed: a chroma key saved into a pipeline document lost its source video resolution, and restored as a block that never worked.** `ChromaKeySettingsX.SourceVideoResolution` is the one setting the keying filter cannot do without - it builds its video format from it - and it was read-only, so it was never written into a saved document and every restored chroma key came back with no resolution at all. Materialization reported success, and the failure showed up only when the pipeline was started: the block could not be built, and a block that cannot be built stops the whole pipeline from starting, with nothing in the diagnostics to say why. The property is now settable and round-trips, and `ChromaKeyBlock` refuses settings whose resolution is missing or has a zero extent, so such a document is reported as failing with `MBS040` naming the property instead of restoring a dead block (#1319).

* [Media Blocks SDK .Net] **The OpenCV blocks now work on macOS.** All sixteen blocks in the `VisioForge.Core.MediaBlocks.OpenCV` namespace, `VideoStabilizationBlock` among them, were documented as cross-platform but their GStreamer plugin shipped for Windows only, so on macOS every one of them reported `IsAvailable()` as `false`.
